I’m just not there right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that might be considered an abnormal or unrealistic concern by others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I can see how other people might think it’s unrealistic. I mean, I came in because I can’t stop focusing on the left side of my face looking uneven and puffy, especially on video calls or in salsa photos. Everyone keeps saying they don’t see anything, but to me it still stands out, and it’s starting to affect my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could change your mind about the left side of your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: Maybe. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’ve been really stuck on how my face looks, especially the left side. On video calls, or in photos from salsa, it looks uneven and kind of puffy to me. Other people keep saying they don’t notice anything, but I can’t stop focusing on it, and it’s starting to affect my work and my social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face might actually look normal, and you're just perceiving it as uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s possible, because people keep telling me they don’t see what I’m seeing. But when I’m on video calls or I see salsa photos, the left side of my face still looks uneven and puffy to me, and I can’t stop focusing on it. It’s hard to just believe it’s normal when it feels so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others are mistaken or lying when they tell you that they don't see anything wrong with your face?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think they’re lying. Maybe they just don’t notice it the way I do. But to me, especially on video calls or in salsa photos, the left side looks uneven and puffy, and then I get stuck on it. Even if people say they don’t see anything, I still can’t stop focusing on it, and it’s been affecting work and being around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your face being uneven and puffy is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean... it could be. I know other people keep telling me they don’t see anything wrong, so maybe my perception isn’t matching reality. But when I see myself on video calls or in those salsa photos, the left side of my face really does look uneven and puffy to me, and I get stuck on it. It’s hard to let it go, and it’s been getting in the way of work and being social.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your belief about your face being uneven and puffy might be false, even if it feels true to you?\n\nPatient: Yes... I think it might be false, or at least maybe I’m seeing it in a way other people don’t. Everyone keeps telling me they don’t notice anything, but I still can’t stop focusing on the left side of my face looking uneven and puffy, especially on video calls or in salsa photos. It feels really obvious to me, and it’s starting to affect my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would be possible for you to change your mind about your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’d like to be able to change my mind, but when I see it, it feels very convincing. Maybe with enough reassurance or help I could question it more, but right now it’s hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your face is actually even and not puffy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think there’s a possibility. I mean, people keep telling me they don’t see what I’m seeing, so maybe my face is actually even and not puffy. But I still get stuck looking at the left side, especially on video calls or in salsa photos, and it feels really noticeable to me. It’s hard because it’s starting to affect work and being around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that no matter what anyone else says or does, you will always believe that the left side of your face is uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always. It feels very strong right now, but I can imagine maybe there’s a chance I could see it differently at some point. I’m just not there right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that might be considered an abnormal or unrealistic concern by others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I can see how other people might think it’s unrealistic. I mean, I came in because I can’t stop focusing on the left side of my face looking uneven and puffy, especially on video calls or in salsa photos. Everyone keeps saying they don’t see anything, but to me it still stands out, and it’s starting to affect my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could change your mind about the left side of your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: Maybe. I think if I could really see, somehow, that it looks the same as the other side, that might help. But right now I keep focusing on how uneven and puffy the left side looks, especially on video calls and in salsa photos. Even when people tell me they don’t see it, I still see it, and it’s been getting in the way at work and socially.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence or reassurance could completely change your belief about the left side of your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think reassurance alone completely changes it for me, because I still end up seeing it again. But I also don’t want to say nothing could ever change my mind. I guess I’m unsure what would be enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is based on a fact that you've discovered, or is it just your own personal opinion?\n\nPatient: I guess it feels like a fact when I’m looking at myself, especially on video calls or in salsa photos. Like, I see the left side looking uneven and puffy, and it’s hard for me to ignore.\n\nBut at the same time, everyone keeps telling me they don’t see it, so I know it may be more my own view of it than something obvious to other people. I just can’t stop focusing on it, and it’s been affecting my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy would change even if a doctor or a plastic surgeon told you that there's nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I think it might help a little if a doctor or plastic surgeon said nothing was wrong, but I don’t know if it would fully change it. I’d probably still notice it on video calls or in salsa photos and keep focusing on the left side looking uneven or puffy. Even when people tell me they don’t see anything, I still get stuck on it, and it’s already been affecting my work and social life.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

On video calls, or in photos from salsa, it looks uneven and kind of puffy to me. Other people keep saying they don’t notice anything, but I can’t stop focusing on it, and it’s starting to affect my work and my social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face might actually look normal, and you're just perceiving it as uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s possible, because people keep telling me they don’t see what I’m seeing. But when I’m on video calls or I see salsa photos, the left side of my face still looks uneven and puffy to me, and I can’t stop focusing on it. It’s hard to just believe it’s normal when it feels so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others are mistaken or lying when they tell you that they don't see anything wrong with your face?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think they’re lying. Maybe they just don’t notice it the way I do. But to me, especially on video calls or in salsa photos, the left side looks uneven and puffy, and then I get stuck on it. Even if people say they don’t see anything, I still can’t stop focusing on it, and it’s been affecting work and being around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your face being uneven and puffy is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean... it could be. I know other people keep telling me they don’t see anything wrong, so maybe my perception isn’t matching reality. But when I see myself on video calls or in those salsa photos, the left side of my face really does look uneven and puffy to me, and I get stuck on it. It’s hard to let it go, and it’s been getting in the way of work and being social.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your belief about your face being uneven and puffy might be false, even if it feels true to you?\n\nPatient: Yes... I think it might be false, or at least maybe I’m seeing it in a way other people don’t. Everyone keeps telling me they don’t notice anything, but I still can’t stop focusing on the left side of my face looking uneven and puffy, especially on video calls or in salsa photos. It feels really obvious to me, and it’s starting to affect my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would be possible for you to change your mind about your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’d like to be able to change my mind, but when I see it, it feels very convincing. Maybe with enough reassurance or help I could question it more, but right now it’s hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your face is actually even and not puffy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think there’s a possibility. I mean, people keep telling me they don’t see what I’m seeing, so maybe my face is actually even and not puffy. But I still get stuck looking at the left side, especially on video calls or in salsa photos, and it feels really noticeable to me. It’s hard because it’s starting to affect work and being around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that no matter what anyone else says or does, you will always believe that the left side of your face is uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always. It feels very strong right now, but I can imagine maybe there’s a chance I could see it differently at some point. I’m just not there right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that might be considered an abnormal or unrealistic concern by others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I can see how other people might think it’s unrealistic. Everyone keeps saying they don’t see anything, but to me it still stands out, and it’s starting to affect my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could change your mind about the left side of your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: Maybe. I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain no matter what. I know other people keep telling me they don’t see it, and that makes me question it a little.

I’m just not there right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that might be considered an abnormal or unrealistic concern by others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I can see how other people might think it’s unrealistic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’ve been really stuck on how my face looks, especially the left side. On video calls, or in photos from salsa, it looks uneven and kind of puffy to me. Other people keep saying they don’t notice anything, but I can’t stop focusing on it, and it’s starting to affect my work and my social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face might actually look normal, and you're just perceiving it as uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s possible, because people keep telling me they don’t see what I’m seeing. But when I’m on video calls or I see salsa photos, the left side of my face still looks uneven and puffy to me, and I can’t stop focusing on it. It’s hard to just believe it’s normal when it feels so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others are mistaken or lying when they tell you that they don't see anything wrong with your face?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think they’re lying. Maybe they just don’t notice it the way I do. But to me, especially on video calls or in salsa photos, the left side looks uneven and puffy, and then I get stuck on it. Even if people say they don’t see anything, I still can’t stop focusing on it, and it’s been affecting work and being around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your face being uneven and puffy is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean... it could be. I know other people keep telling me they don’t see anything wrong, so maybe my perception isn’t matching reality. But when I see myself on video calls or in those salsa photos, the left side of my face really does look uneven and puffy to me, and I get stuck on it. It’s hard to let it go, and it’s been getting in the way of work and being social.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your belief about your face being uneven and puffy might be false, even if it feels true to you?\n\nPatient: Yes... I think it might be false, or at least maybe I’m seeing it in a way other people don’t. Everyone keeps telling me they don’t notice anything, but I still can’t stop focusing on the left side of my face looking uneven and puffy, especially on video calls or in salsa photos. It feels really obvious to me, and it’s starting to affect my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would be possible for you to change your mind about your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’d like to be able to change my mind, but when I see it, it feels very convincing. Maybe with enough reassurance or help I could question it more, but right now it’s hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your face is actually even and not puffy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think there’s a possibility. I mean, people keep telling me they don’t see what I’m seeing, so maybe my face is actually even and not puffy. But I still get stuck looking at the left side, especially on video calls or in salsa photos, and it feels really noticeable to me. It’s hard because it’s starting to affect work and being around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that no matter what anyone else says or does, you will always believe that the left side of your face is uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always. It feels very strong right now, but I can imagine maybe there’s a chance I could see it differently at some point. I’m just not there right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that might be considered an abnormal or unrealistic concern by others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I can see how other people might think it’s unrealistic. I mean, I came in because I can’t stop focusing on the left side of my face looking uneven and puffy, especially on video calls or in salsa photos. Everyone keeps saying they don’t see anything, but to me it still stands out, and it’s starting to affect my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could change your mind about the left side of your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: Maybe. I think if I could really see, somehow, that it looks the same as the other side, that might help. But right now I keep focusing on how uneven and puffy the left side looks, especially on video calls and in salsa photos. Even when people tell me they don’t see it, I still see it, and it’s been getting in the way at work and socially.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence or reassurance could completely change your belief about the left side of your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think reassurance alone completely changes it for me, because I still end up seeing it again. But I also don’t want to say nothing could ever change my mind. I guess I’m unsure what would be enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is based on a fact that you've discovered, or is it just your own personal opinion?\n\nPatient: I guess it feels like a fact when I’m looking at myself, especially on video calls or in salsa photos. Like, I see the left side looking uneven and puffy, and it’s hard for me to ignore.\n\nBut at the same time, everyone keeps telling me they don’t see it, so I know it may be more my own view of it than something obvious to other people. I just can’t stop focusing on it, and it’s been affecting my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy would change even if a doctor or a plastic surgeon told you that there's nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I think it might help a little if a doctor or plastic surgeon said nothing was wrong, but I don’t know if it would fully change it. I’d probably still notice it on video calls or in salsa photos and keep focusing on the left side looking uneven or puffy. Even when people tell me they don’t see anything, I still get stuck on it, and it’s already been affecting my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or any information you could receive that would completely convince you that your face is not uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would completely convince me. Even when people tell me they don’t see anything, I still catch it on video calls or in salsa photos and then I’m right back to focusing on the left side of my face.\n\nI guess really clear proof might help, but honestly I came in because I can’t stop noticing it, and it’s starting to interfere with work and my social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that you are absolutely certain about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain no matter what. I know other people keep telling me they don’t see it, and that makes me question it a little.\n\nBut when I’m on video calls or I see salsa photos, I get really focused on the left side of my face looking uneven and puffy, and it feels very real in that moment. That’s why I came in — I can’t seem to stop focusing on it, and it’s starting to affect my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that could be considered a delusion by mental health professionals?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a delusion. I mean, I know other people keep saying they don’t see anything, so part of me understands that maybe I’m seeing it more than they are.\n\nBut I came in because I can’t stop focusing on how uneven and puffy the left side of my face looks, especially on video calls and in salsa photos. It feels real to me when I see it, and it’s starting to mess with my work and my social life.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Maybe with enough reassurance or help I could question it more, but right now it’s hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your face is actually even and not puffy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think there’s a possibility. I mean, people keep telling me they don’t see what I’m seeing, so maybe my face is actually even and not puffy. But I still get stuck looking at the left side, especially on video calls or in salsa photos, and it feels really noticeable to me. It’s hard because it’s starting to affect work and being around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that no matter what anyone else says or does, you will always believe that the left side of your face is uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always. It feels very strong right now, but I can imagine maybe there’s a chance I could see it differently at some point. I’m just not there right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that might be considered an abnormal or unrealistic concern by others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I can see how other people might think it’s unrealistic. I mean, I came in because I can’t stop focusing on the left side of my face looking uneven and puffy, especially on video calls or in salsa photos. Everyone keeps saying they don’t see anything, but to me it still stands out, and it’s starting to affect my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could change your mind about the left side of your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: Maybe. I think if I could really see, somehow, that it looks the same as the other side, that might help. But right now I keep focusing on how uneven and puffy the left side looks, especially on video calls and in salsa photos. Even when people tell me they don’t see it, I still see it, and it’s been getting in the way at work and socially.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence or reassurance could completely change your belief about the left side of your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think reassurance alone completely changes it for me, because I still end up seeing it again. But I also don’t want to say nothing could ever change my mind. I guess I’m unsure what would be enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is based on a fact that you've discovered, or is it just your own personal opinion?\n\nPatient: I guess it feels like a fact when I’m looking at myself, especially on video calls or in salsa photos. Like, I see the left side looking uneven and puffy, and it’s hard for me to ignore.\n\nBut at the same time, everyone keeps telling me they don’t see it, so I know it may be more my own view of it than something obvious to other people. I just can’t stop focusing on it, and it’s been affecting my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy would change even if a doctor or a plastic surgeon told you that there's nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I think it might help a little if a doctor or plastic surgeon said nothing was wrong, but I don’t know if it would fully change it. I’d probably still notice it on video calls or in salsa photos and keep focusing on the left side looking uneven or puffy. Even when people tell me they don’t see anything, I still get stuck on it, and it’s already been affecting my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or any information you could receive that would completely convince you that your face is not uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would completely convince me. Even when people tell me they don’t see anything, I still catch it on video calls or in salsa photos and then I’m right back to focusing on the left side of my face.\n\nI guess really clear proof might help, but honestly I came in because I can’t stop noticing it, and it’s starting to interfere with work and my social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that you are absolutely certain about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain no matter what. I know other people keep telling me they don’t see it, and that makes me question it a little.\n\nBut when I’m on video calls or I see salsa photos, I get really focused on the left side of my face looking uneven and puffy, and it feels very real in that moment. That’s why I came in — I can’t seem to stop focusing on it, and it’s starting to affect my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that could be considered a delusion by mental health professionals?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a delusion. I mean, I know other people keep saying they don’t see anything, so part of me understands that maybe I’m seeing it more than they are.\n\nBut I came in because I can’t stop focusing on how uneven and puffy the left side of my face looks, especially on video calls and in salsa photos. It comes back whenever I notice it again.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Even when people tell me they don’t see it, I still see it, and it’s been getting in the way at work and socially.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence or reassurance could completely change your belief about the left side of your face being uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think reassurance alone completely changes it for me, because I still end up seeing it again. But I also don’t want to say nothing could ever change my mind. I guess I’m unsure what would be enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is based on a fact that you've discovered, or is it just your own personal opinion?\n\nPatient: I guess it feels like a fact when I’m looking at myself, especially on video calls or in salsa photos. Like, I see the left side looking uneven and puffy, and it’s hard for me to ignore.\n\nBut at the same time, everyone keeps telling me they don’t see it, so I know it may be more my own view of it than something obvious to other people. I just can’t stop focusing on it, and it’s been affecting my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy would change even if a doctor or a plastic surgeon told you that there's nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I think it might help a little if a doctor or plastic surgeon said nothing was wrong, but I don’t know if it would fully change it. I’d probably still notice it on video calls or in salsa photos and keep focusing on the left side looking uneven or puffy. Even when people tell me they don’t see anything, I still get stuck on it, and it’s already been affecting my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or any information you could receive that would completely convince you that your face is not uneven and puffy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would completely convince me. Even when people tell me they don’t see anything, I still catch it on video calls or in salsa photos and then I’m right back to focusing on the left side of my face.\n\nI guess really clear proof might help, but honestly I came in because I can’t stop noticing it, and it’s starting to interfere with work and my social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that you are absolutely certain about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain no matter what. I know other people keep telling me they don’t see it, and that makes me question it a little.\n\nBut when I’m on video calls or I see salsa photos, I get really focused on the left side of my face looking uneven and puffy, and it feels very real in that moment. That’s why I came in — I can’t seem to stop focusing on it, and it’s starting to affect my work and social life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being uneven and puffy is something that could be considered a delusion by mental health professionals?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a delusion. I mean, I know other people keep saying they don’t see anything, so part of me understands that maybe I’m seeing it more than they are.\n\nBut I came in because I can’t stop focusing on how uneven and puffy the left side of my face looks, especially on video calls and in salsa photos.
